\nu\mapsto\nu+i\tfrac{\pi}{\beta}
for
t<0
a straigtforward calculation yields
|g(t)|\leq\sigma_{E}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\exp\!\left(\frac{\pi^{2}}{8\beta^{2}\sigma_{E}^{2}}\right)e^{-\tfrac{\pi}{\beta}|t|}.
From this and
\kappa\in(0,1/2),
another straightforward calculation yields (
4.20
). Note that
C_{\beta,r,\kappa}<\infty
for every
\beta>0
and
r\geq 0
.

(4.20)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}|g(t)|\,e^{r|t|^{2\kappa}/2}\,dt\;\leq C_{\beta,r,\kappa}\,\sigma_{E}\,\,\exp\!\left(\frac{\pi^{2}}{8\beta^{2}\sigma_{E}^{2}}\right),\,

for some finite and
\beta,r
and
\kappa
dependent constant
C_{\beta,r,\kappa}\geq 0.

Similarly, we obtain that

\displaystyle\|\rho(G^{\dagger}_{\sigma_{E}}-\left(G^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E}}\right)^{\dagger})\|_{1}\leq C_{\beta,r,\kappa}C^{2}_{f}C_{A}\,|\mathcal{A}|\,\sigma_{E}\exp\!\left(\tfrac{\pi^{2}}{8\beta^{2}\sigma_{E}^{2}}\right)q(M)e^{-M^{\kappa}}\sqrt{E_{4}}.

Next, for

(4.21)

\displaystyle X^{\alpha}_{s}:=e^{isH}L^{\alpha}e^{-isH},\quad X^{\alpha,\leq M}_{s}:=e^{isH}L^{\alpha,\leq M}e^{-isH}

we get for any
s\in\mathbb{R}
, using an analogous argument to (
4.17
) and the fact that
|t+s|^{2\kappa}\leq|t|^{2\kappa}+|s|^{2\kappa}
that

\displaystyle\|X^{\alpha}_{s}\sqrt{\rho}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{A}\sqrt{E_{2}}\int|f(t)|e^{r|t+s|^{2\kappa}/2}dt\leq C_{A}C_{f}\sqrt{E_{2}}\,e^{r|s|^{2\kappa}/2}

\displaystyle\|X^{\alpha,\leq M}_{s}\sqrt{\rho}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{A}\sqrt{E_{2}}\int|f(t)|e^{r|t+s|^{2\kappa}/2}dt\leq C_{A}C_{f}\sqrt{E_{2}}\,e^{r|s|^{2\kappa}/2}

and furthermore, by an analogous argument to (
4.15
) that

\displaystyle\left\|(X^{\alpha}_{s}-X^{\alpha,\leq M}_{s})\sqrt{\rho}\right\|_{2}\leq C_{f}\,q(M)e^{-M^{\kappa}}\sqrt{E_{2}}\,e^{r|s|^{2\kappa}/2}

which in total gives

\displaystyle\left\|X^{\alpha}_{s}\rho(X^{\alpha})_{s}^{\dagger}-X^{\alpha,\leq M}_{s}\rho(X^{\alpha,\leq M}_{s})^{\dagger}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\|(X^{\alpha}_{s}-X^{\alpha,\leq M}_{s})\sqrt{\rho}\|_{2}\|\sqrt{\rho}(X^{\alpha}_{s})^{\dagger}\|_{2}+\|X^{\alpha,\leq M}_{s}\sqrt{\rho}\|_{2}\|\sqrt{\rho}((X^{\alpha}_{s})^{\dagger}-(X^{\alpha,\leq M}_{s})^{\dagger})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 2C_{A}C^{2}_{f}\,E_{2}e^{r|s|^{2\kappa}}q(M)e^{-M^{\kappa}}\,

Therefore, denoting the map

(4.22)

\displaystyle\Phi^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}(\rho):=\sigma_{E}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-2\sigma_{E}^{2}s^{2}}X^{\alpha,\leq M}_{s}\rho(X^{\alpha,\leq M}_{s})^{\dagger}ds,

we get that

\displaystyle\|(\Phi^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}-\Phi_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H})(\rho)\|_{1}\leq 4|\mathcal{A}|C_{A}C^{2}_{f}\,E_{2}q(M)e^{-M^{\kappa}}\exp\left(r+\tfrac{r^{2}}{8\sigma^{2}_{E}}\right),

where we used that

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\,\sigma_{E}\int e^{-2\sigma^{2}_{E}s^{2}}e^{r|s|^{2\kappa}}ds\leq e^{r}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\,\sigma_{E}\int e^{-2\sigma^{2}_{E}s^{2}}e^{r|s|}ds\leq 2\exp\left(r+\tfrac{r^{2}}{8\sigma^{2}_{E}}\right).

∎

To end this section, we show that the mean field Bose Hubbard Hamiltonian,

H_{\text{mfBH}}=-\mu N+\tfrac{U}{2}\tfrac{N(N-1)}{2}-\overline{\psi}a-\psi a^{\dagger}+|\psi|^{2},

satisfies the assumption (
4.13
). More generally, we consider one-mode Hamiltonians of the form

\displaystyle H=h(N)+\overline{\psi}a+\psi a^{\dagger}

for some function
h:\mathbb{N}_{0}\to\mathbb{R}
and
\psi\in\mathbb{C}.
In the following Lemma we find that for
\kappa\in[0,1/2]
and
k\geq 0
these Hamiltonians are energy limited with respect to
e^{kN^{\kappa}}.

Lemma 4.9
.

Let
\psi\in\mathbb{C}
and let
H=h(N)+\overline{\psi}a+\psi a^{\dagger}
have a self-adjoint realization for which the finite Fock span is an operator core.
Then for
0\leq\kappa\leq 1/2
and
k\geq 0
and
t\in\mathbb{R}
we have

(4.23)

\displaystyle e^{itH}e^{kN^{\kappa}}e^{-itH}\leq e^{r|t|^{2\kappa}}e^{kN^{\kappa}}

for some
r\geq 0
depending only on
k,\kappa
and
\psi.

Proof.

The cases
k=0
and
\kappa=0
are immediate. The following form estimates are justified by finite Fock compressions and closure.
We consider
G(t):=e^{itH}e^{k(N+1+t^{2})^{\kappa}}e^{-itH}
and differentiate

(4.24)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}G(t)

\displaystyle=2k\kappa te^{itH}(N+1+t^{2})^{\kappa-1}e^{k(N+1+t^{2})^{\kappa}}e^{-itH}+ie^{itH}\left[H,e^{k(N+1+t^{2})^{\kappa}}\right]e^{-itH}.

In the following we estimate both operators appearing on the right hand side respectively. For the first term we use
\kappa-1\leq 0
which gives

\displaystyle 2k\kappa|t|\langle\psi,e^{itH}(N+1+t^{2})^{\kappa-1}e^{k(N+1+t^{2})^{\kappa}}e^{-itH}\psi\rangle

\displaystyle\leq 2k|t|^{2\kappa-1}\langle\psi,e^{itH}e^{k(N+1+t^{2})^{\kappa}}e^{-itH}\psi\rangle

(4.25)

\displaystyle\leq 2k|t|^{2\kappa-1}\langle\psi,G(t)\psi\rangle

For the second term we use

(4.26)

\displaystyle\left[H,e^{k(N+1+t^{2})^{\kappa}}\right]

\displaystyle=\overline{\psi}\left(e^{k(N+2+t^{2})^{\kappa}}-e^{k(N+1+t^{2})^{\kappa}}\right)a+\psi\left(e^{k(N+t^{2})^{\kappa}}-e^{k(N+1+t^{2})^{\kappa}}\right)a^{\dagger}

and bound each term individually. For the first term we expand
\ket{\varphi}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\varphi_{n}\ket{n}
and note

\displaystyle|\bra{\varphi}\left(e^{k(N+2+t^{2})^{\kappa}}-e^{k(N+1+t^{2})^{\kappa}}\right)a\ket{\varphi}|\leq\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}|\varphi_{n}||\varphi_{n-1}|\sqrt{n}\left|e^{k(n+1+t^{2})^{\kappa}}-e^{k(n+t^{2})^{\kappa}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq k\kappa\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}|\varphi_{n}||\varphi_{n-1}|\sqrt{n}(n+t^{2})^{\kappa-1}e^{k(n+1+t^{2})^{\kappa}}\leq k\kappa|t|^{2\kappa-1}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}|\varphi_{n}||\varphi_{n-1}|e^{k(n+1+t^{2})^{\kappa}}

\displaystyle\leq C|t|^{2\kappa-1}\bra{\varphi}e^{k(N+1+t^{2})^{\kappa}}\ket{\varphi},

for some
C\geq 0
which depends on
k
and
\kappa
but is independent of all other variables and where we have used in the second inequality we have used the mean value theorem, in the third the fact that
\kappa\leq 1/2
and in the fourth the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality. The second term in (
4.26
) can be treated similarly giving the bound

\displaystyle|\bra{\varphi}\left(e^{k(N+1+t^{2})^{\kappa}}-e^{k(N+t^{2})^{\kappa}}\right)a^{\dagger}\ket{\varphi}|\leq C|t|^{2\kappa-1}\bra{\varphi}e^{k(N+1+t^{2})^{\kappa}}\ket{\varphi}.

Combining these estimates with (
4.24
) and (
4.25
) we get

\displaystyle-C^{\prime}|t|^{2\kappa-1}G(t)\leq\frac{d}{dt}G(t)\leq C^{\prime}|t|^{2\kappa-1}G(t)

for some constant
C^{\prime}\geq 0
depending only on
k,\kappa
and
\psi.
Using Grönwall’s inequality in the case
t\geq 0
on the interval
[0,t]
or for the case
t\leq 0
on the interval
[0,-t]
proves

\displaystyle G(t)\leq e^{r|t|^{2\kappa}}G(0)=e^{r|t|^{2\kappa}}e^{k(N+1)^{\kappa}}

for some
r\geq 0
depending again only on
k,\kappa
and
\psi.
Using
e^{kN^{\kappa}}\leq e^{k(N+1+t^{2})^{\kappa}}
and
(N+1)^{\kappa}\leq N^{\kappa}+1
gives
|t|\geq 1
; the same commutator estimate with
e^{kN^{\kappa}}
gives
|t|\leq 1
.
∎

4.3.2.
Truncating the Hamiltonian

In the following we provide a bounded Lindbladian with a finite-dimensional system-register restriction , denoted by
\mathcal{L}^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}},
which is good approximation of the generator
\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}
studied in Section
4.1
and
4.2
. For that we consider the finite-dimensional approximations,
\{(A^{\alpha})^{\leq M}\}_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}},
of the bare jumps
\{A^{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}
analysed in Section
4.3
and replace the Hamiltonian
H
within the corresponding generator
\mathcal{L}^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}
by its finite-dimensional truncation,
H_{\leq M},
to obtain
\mathcal{L}^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}.
As in Section
4.3
we have already seen that
\mathcal{L}^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}
is a good approximation of
\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}
on certain energy constraint input states, we focus in this section on the approximation of
\mathcal{L}^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}
by the fully finite-dimensional
8
8

8

On the original Hilbert space ,
\mathcal{L}^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}
is a bounded generator , generally not a finite-rank superoperator . Its restriction to the reducing system register is finite dimensional .
generator
\mathcal{L}^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}.

For that, we start with defining the truncated, finite rank Hamiltonian
H_{\leq M}
for some self-adjoint, possibly unbounded, Hamiltonian
H
on some separable Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}:
More precisely we consider a parametrised family of self-adjoint and finite rank projections
\left(P_{M}\right)_{M\in\mathbb{N}}
on
\mathcal{H}
such that
P_{M}\mathcal{H}\subseteq D(H)
and with that the finite rank truncation

(4.27)

\displaystyle H_{\leq M}:=P_{M}HP_{M}\qquad\text{ with }\qquad\|H_{\leq M}\|\leq p_{5}(|\mathcal{A}|,M)

for some polynomial
p_{5}
of the number,
|\mathcal{A}|,
of jumps and truncation
M
. As described in the Introduction, we refer to the finite dimensional space
\operatorname{im}(P_{M})
as the
system register
. In many-body or multi-mode systems, one usually considers system registers whose dimensions satisfies

\log\left(\dim\bigl(\operatorname{im}(P_{M})\bigr)\right)=\mathcal{O}\bigl(|\mathcal{A}|\log(M)\bigr),
but we are in principle free to leave the dimension of the system register unspecified at this stage.

We assume in the following that
H
is well-approximated by
H_{\leq M}
on energy constraint input states; specifically, we have for some
\kappa\in(0,1/2)

(4.28)

\displaystyle\left\|\left(H-H_{\leq M}\right)e^{-N_{\mathcal{A}}^{\kappa}}\right\|\leq p(|\mathcal{A}|,M)e^{-M^{\kappa}},

where
p(|\mathcal{A}|,M)
is some polynomially bounded function and
N_{\mathcal{A}}
is some self-adjoint and positive semidefinite operator.

Let us illustrate the above with the example of a
m
-mode bosonic system on the Hilbert space
\left(L^{2}(\mathbb{R})\right)^{\otimes m},
with multi-index
\alpha
taken from the set
\mathcal{A}=\{(i,+),(i,-)\}_{i=1}^{m}
and bare jumps
\{A^{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}=\left\{a^{k}_{i},(a_{i}^{k})^{\dagger}\right\}_{i=1}^{m}
for some
k\in\mathbb{N}:

In this case, we take
P_{M}=\pi^{\otimes m}_{M}
with
\pi_{M}=\sum_{n=0}^{M}|n\rangle\!\langle n|
being the local truncations onto the first
M+1
Fock states of a fixed mode. Furthermore, for this multimode case, we consider
N_{\mathcal{A}}\equiv N_{\operatorname{tot}}:=\sum_{i=1}^{m}a^{\dagger}_{i}a_{i}
such that (
4.28
) is naturally satisfied for Hamiltonians
H
being polynomials in
a_{i}
and
a^{\dagger}_{i}
with degree
\mathcal{O}(1)
in the number of modes
m
, e.g., the Bose-Hubbard Hamiltonian.

Additionally to (
4.28
), we assume as in Section
4.3
that
H
satisfies (
4.13
), i.e. that for
k=2,4
and
\kappa\in(0,1/2)
as above there exists
r\geq 0
such that for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
we have
9
9

9

The reason for the choice of range of
\kappa,
in particular the constraint
\kappa<1/2
, is that this enables us to prove approximation of
\mathcal{L}^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}
by
\mathcal{L}^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}
for all
\beta>0.
The reason for this is that the function
e^{r|t|^{2\kappa}/2}
needs to be dominated by the function
g(t),
which is featured in the integral representations of the generators stated in Proposition
4.4
, and it can be shown that
|g(t)|\lesssim e^{-\tfrac{\pi}{\beta}|t|}
for large
t.

(4.29)

\displaystyle e^{-itH}e^{kN_{\mathcal{A}}^{\kappa}}e^{itH}\leq e^{r|t|^{2\kappa}}\,e^{kN_{\mathcal{A}}^{\kappa}}.

Under both of these assumptions, we see in the following lemma that the unitary evolutions of the unbounded and truncated Hamiltonians are close on energy constraint inputs:

Following the notation of Section
4.3
, we show in the following that under the assumptions (
4.29
) and (
4.28
) the bounded generator with finite-dimensional register restriction ,
\mathcal{L}^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}},
is close to
\mathcal{L}^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}.

For this, we first prove the following technical Lemma. In what follows, we denote for some function
f\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R})
the integrated jump operators

(4.30)

\displaystyle L^{\alpha,\leq M}:=\int f(s)e^{isH}(A^{\alpha})^{\leq M}e^{-isH}ds\quad\text{and}\quad L^{\alpha,\leq M}_{\leq M}:=\int f(s)e^{isH_{\leq M}}(A^{\alpha})^{\leq M}e^{-isH_{\leq M}}ds.

By construction, we have that

(4.31)

\displaystyle\|L^{\alpha,\leq M}_{\leq M}\|\leq\|f\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R})}\|(A^{\alpha})^{\leq M}\|\leq\|f\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R})}\,q^{\prime}(M),

where the polynomial
q^{\prime}(M)
is defined in (
4.10
).

Lemma 4.10
.

Let
f\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R})
and assume that
Condition

A
, (
4.10
) and (
4.12
) are satisfied for the set of bare jumps
\{A^{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}
. Furthermore, for
N_{\mathcal{A}}
self-adjoint and positive semidefinite assume that (
4.28
) and (
4.29
) holds for some
\kappa\in(0,1/2)
and
r\geq 0
and that

\displaystyle C^{\prime}_{f}:=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|t|e^{r|t|^{2\kappa}}|f(t)|dt<\infty.

Then for
M\in\mathbb{N}
we have

\displaystyle\|(L^{\alpha,\leq M}\!-\!L^{\alpha,\leq M}_{\leq M})e^{-N_{\mathcal{A}}^{\kappa}}\|\!

\displaystyle\leq\!C^{\prime}_{f}p_{1}(|\mathcal{A}|,M)e^{-M^{\kappa}},

\displaystyle\|((L^{\alpha,\leq M})^{\dagger}L^{\alpha,\leq M}-\left(L^{\alpha,\leq M}_{\leq M}\right)^{\dagger}L^{\alpha,\leq M}_{\leq M})e^{-N_{\mathcal{A}}^{\kappa}}\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{f}C^{\prime}_{f}p_{2}(|\mathcal{A}|,M)e^{-M^{\kappa}},\,

where
p_{1}(|\mathcal{A}|,M)=2q^{\prime}(M)p(|\mathcal{A}|,M)
and
p_{2}(|\mathcal{A}|,M)=4(q^{\prime}(M))^{2}p(|\mathcal{A}|,M)
with
q^{\prime}(M)
being the polynomially bounded function appearing in (
4.10
) and (
4.12
) and
C_{f}
being defined in (
4.14
).

Proof.

We first record the Hamiltonian estimate needed here and below.
Duhamel’s formula on the weighted domain, justified first on the
operator domain and then by the assumed weighted bounds, together with

(
4.28
)
and
(
4.29
)
, gives

(4.32)

\displaystyle\left\|(e^{-itH}-e^{-itH_{\leq M}})e^{-N_{\mathcal{A}}^{\kappa}}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{|t|}e^{rs^{2\kappa}/2}\,ds\,\left\|(H-H_{\leq M})e^{-N_{\mathcal{A}}^{\kappa}}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq|t|e^{r|t|^{2\kappa}/2}p(|\mathcal{A}|,M)e^{-M^{\kappa}}.

For the first inequality in Lemma
4.10
we use (
4.29
) and (
4.32
) to see for
\ket{\psi}\in D\left(e^{N_{\mathcal{A}}^{\kappa}}\right)
that we have

\displaystyle\|(L^{\alpha,\leq M}-L^{\alpha,\leq M}_{\leq M})\ket{\psi}\|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\int|f(s)|\Big(\left\|(A^{\alpha})^{\leq M}\left(e^{-isH}-e^{-isH_{\leq M}}\right)\ket{\psi}\right\|+\left\|\left(e^{isH}-e^{isH_{\leq M}}\right)(A^{\alpha})^{\leq M}e^{-isH}\ket{\psi}\right\|\Big)ds

(4.33)

\displaystyle\quad\leq 2q^{\prime}(M)p(|\mathcal{A}|,M)e^{-M^{\kappa}}\int|f(s)||s|e^{r|s|^{2\kappa}}ds\|e^{N_{\mathcal{A}}^{\kappa}}\ket{\psi}\|=2C^{\prime}_{f}q^{\prime}(M)p(|\mathcal{A}|,M)e^{-M^{\kappa}}\|e^{N_{\mathcal{A}}^{\kappa}}\ket{\psi}\|,

where we used (
4.10
) and (
4.12
) in the second inequality.

Next, we control squares of the
L
operators for
\ket{\varphi}\in D(e^{N_{\mathcal{A}}^{\kappa}})
:

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\left(L^{\alpha,\leq M}\right)^{\dagger}L^{\alpha,\leq M}-\left(L^{\alpha,\leq M}_{\leq M}\right)^{\dagger}L^{\alpha,\leq M}_{\leq M}\right)\ket{\varphi}\right\|

(4.34)

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\left(L^{\alpha,\leq M}_{\leq M}\right)^{\dagger}(L^{\alpha,\leq M}-L^{\alpha,\leq M}_{\leq M})\ket{\varphi}\right\|+\left\|\left(\left(L^{\alpha,\leq M}\right)^{\dagger}-\left(L^{\alpha,\leq M}_{\leq M}\right)^{\dagger}\right)L^{\alpha,\leq M}\ket{\varphi}\right\|.

The first term above can be controlled by using that by (
4.10
) we have
\left\|\left(L_{\leq M}^{\alpha,\leq M}\right)^{\dagger}\right\|\leq q^{\prime}(M)\|f\|_{1}\leq q^{\prime}(M)C_{f}
and therefore using (
4.33
) we have

\displaystyle\left\|\left(L^{\alpha,\leq M}_{\leq M}\right)^{\dagger}(L^{\alpha,\leq M}-L^{\alpha,\leq M}_{\leq M})\ket{\varphi}\right\|\leq 2C_{f}C^{\prime}_{f}(q^{\prime}(M))^{2}p(|\mathcal{A}|,M)e^{-M^{\kappa}}\|e^{N_{\mathcal{A}}^{\kappa}}\ket{\varphi}\|.

To control the second term in (
4.34
), we note that by (
4.29
) and (
4.12
) we have

\displaystyle\left\|e^{N_{\mathcal{A}}^{\kappa}}L^{\alpha,\leq M}e^{-N_{\mathcal{A}}^{\kappa}}\right\|\leq\int|f(s)|e^{r|s|^{2\kappa}/2}\left\|e^{N_{\mathcal{A}}^{\kappa}}e^{isH}(A^{\alpha})^{\leq M}e^{-N_{\mathcal{A}}^{\kappa}}\right\|ds\,

(4.35)

\displaystyle\leq q^{\prime}(M)\int|f(s)|e^{r|s|^{2\kappa}}ds=q^{\prime}(M)C_{f},

where
C_{f}
is defined in (
4.14
). From this
and the adjoint version of (
4.33
) we bound the second term in (
4.34
) as

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\left(L^{\alpha,\leq M}\right)^{\dagger}-\left(L^{\alpha,\leq M}_{\leq M}\right)^{\dagger}\right)L^{\alpha,\leq M}\ket{\varphi}\right\|\leq 2C_{f}C^{\prime}_{f}(q^{\prime}(M))^{2}p(|\mathcal{A}|,M)e^{-M^{\kappa}}\left\|e^{N_{\mathcal{A}}^{\kappa}}\ket{\varphi}\right\|,

which finishes the proof.
∎

We are now ready to show in the following proposition that
\mathcal{L}^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}
can be well approximated by the bounded generator
\mathcal{L}^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}
, whose system-register restriction is finite dimensional.

Proposition 4.11
.

Let
f\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R})
and assume that
Condition

A
, (
4.10
) and (
4.12
) are satisfied for the set of bare jumps
\{A^{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}
. Furthermore, for
N_{\mathcal{A}}
self-adjoint and positive semidefinite assume that (
4.28
) and (
4.29
) holds for some
\kappa\in(0,1/2)
and
r\geq 0
and that

\displaystyle C^{\prime}_{f}:=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|t|e^{r|t|^{2\kappa}}|f(t)|dt<\infty.

Then, for all
\beta,\,\sigma_{E}>0,

M\in\mathbb{N}
and state
\rho
satisfying
E_{2}:=\operatorname{Tr}(e^{2N_{\mathcal{A}}^{\kappa}}\rho)<\infty
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|(\mathcal{L}^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}-\mathcal{L}^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}})(\rho)\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C_{\beta,r,\kappa}\widetilde{C}_{f}\sqrt{E_{2}}\,p_{3}(|\mathcal{A}|,M)e^{-M^{\kappa}}\left(\sigma_{E}\exp\!\left(\tfrac{\pi^{2}}{8\beta^{2}\sigma_{E}^{2}}\right)+\exp\left(r+\tfrac{(r+1)^{2}}{8\sigma^{2}_{E}}\right)\right),

for some constant
C_{\beta,r,\kappa}\geq 0
and
where
p_{3}(|\mathcal{A}|,M):=(q^{\prime}(M))^{2}|\mathcal{A}|\,p(|\mathcal{A}|,M)
is polynomially bounded,
\widetilde{C}_{f}:=C_{f}\max\{C_{f},C^{\prime}_{f}\}
and
C_{f}
is defined in (
4.14
).

Proof.

We make use of the integral representation of Proposition
4.4
. First, we denote the operators

\displaystyle G^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E}}\!

\displaystyle:=\!-\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\!\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\!\!\!\!\!g(t)e^{itH}(L^{\alpha,\leq M})^{\dagger}L^{\alpha,\leq M}e^{-itH}\!dt

\displaystyle G^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\leq M}\!

\displaystyle:=\!-\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\!\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\!\!\!\!\!g(t)e^{itH_{\leq M}}(L_{\leq M}^{\alpha,\leq M})^{\dagger}L_{\leq M}^{\alpha,\leq M}e^{-itH_{\leq M}}\!dt,

where
G^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E}}
appears in the definition of
\mathcal{L}^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}
and
G^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\leq M}
appears in the definition of
\mathcal{L}^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}.

We see

\displaystyle\left\|\left(G^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E}}-G^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\leq M}\right)\rho\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\int|g(t)|\left\|\left(e^{itH}-e^{itH_{\leq M}}\right)(L^{\alpha,\leq M})^{\dagger}L^{\alpha,\leq M}e^{-itH}dt\,\rho\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\int|g(t)|\left\|\left((L^{\alpha,\leq M})^{\dagger}L^{\alpha,\leq M}-(L^{\alpha,\leq M}_{\leq M})^{\dagger}L^{\alpha,\leq M}_{\leq M}\right)e^{-itH}dt\,\rho\right\|_{1}

(4.36)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\int|g(t)|\left\|(L^{\alpha,\leq M}_{\leq M})^{\dagger}L^{\alpha,\leq M}_{\leq M}\left(e^{-itH}-e^{-itH_{\leq M}}\right)dt\,\rho\right\|_{1}.

For the first term we note using (
4.29
) and (
4.12
)
together with an analogous argument as for (
4.35
) that

\displaystyle\left\|e^{N_{\mathcal{A}}^{\kappa}}(L^{\alpha,\leq M})^{\dagger}L^{\alpha,\leq M}e^{-N_{\mathcal{A}}^{\kappa}}\right\|\leq\left\|e^{N_{\mathcal{A}}^{\kappa}}(L^{\alpha,\leq M})^{\dagger}e^{-N_{\mathcal{A}}^{\kappa}}\right\|\left\|e^{N_{\mathcal{A}}^{\kappa}}L^{\alpha,\leq M}e^{-N_{\mathcal{A}}^{\kappa}}\right\|\leq C^{2}_{f}(q^{\prime}(M))^{2}

and therefore using (
4.32
)

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\int|g(t)|\left\|\left(e^{itH}-e^{itH_{\leq M}}\right)(L^{\alpha,\leq M})^{\dagger}L^{\alpha,\leq M}e^{-itH}dt\,\rho\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C^{2}_{f}|\mathcal{A}|(q^{\prime}(M))^{2}\sqrt{E_{2}}\int|g(t)|e^{r|t|^{2\kappa}/2}\left\|\left(e^{itH}-e^{itH_{\leq M}}\right)e^{-N_{\mathcal{A}}^{\kappa}}\right\|dt

\displaystyle\leq C^{2}_{f}|\mathcal{A}|(q^{\prime}(M))^{2}\sqrt{E_{2}}\,p(|\mathcal{A}|,M)e^{-M^{\kappa}}\int|g(t)||t|e^{r|t|^{2\kappa}}dt

\displaystyle\leq C_{\beta,r,\kappa}C^{2}_{f}\sqrt{E_{2}}\,\sigma_{E}\,\,\exp\!\left(\frac{\pi^{2}}{8\beta^{2}\sigma_{E}^{2}}\right)|\mathcal{A}|(q^{\prime}(M))^{2}p(|\mathcal{A}|,M)e^{-M^{\kappa}}

for some constant
C_{\beta,r,\kappa}\geq 0
and
where in the last inequality we used (
4.20
) with a larger value of
r
in there. The third term in (
4.36
) can be estimated analogously and the second as well utilising additionally Lemma
4.10
which in total gives

\displaystyle\left\|\left(G^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E}}-G^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\leq M}\right)\rho\right\|_{1}\leq C_{\beta,r,\kappa}\widetilde{C}_{f}\sqrt{E_{2}}\,\sigma_{E}\,\,\exp\!\left(\frac{\pi^{2}}{8\beta^{2}\sigma_{E}^{2}}\right)|\mathcal{A}|p_{2}(|\mathcal{A}|,M)e^{-M^{\kappa}},

where we increased the constant
C_{\beta,r,\kappa}
by a factor 3 compared to the above and, furthermore, denoted
\widetilde{C}_{f}:=C_{f}\max\{C_{f},C^{\prime}_{f}\}.

Next, for

\displaystyle X^{\alpha,\leq M}_{s}:=e^{isH}L^{\alpha,\leq M}e^{-isH},\quad X^{\alpha,\leq M}_{s,\leq M}:=e^{isH_{\leq M}}L^{\alpha,\leq M}_{\leq M}e^{-is{H_{\leq M}}}

using that by (
4.10
) we have
\|X^{\alpha,\leq M}_{s}\|,\,\|X^{\alpha,\leq M}_{s,\leq M}\|\leq\|f\|_{1}q^{\prime}(M)\leq C_{f}q^{\prime}(M),

we see

\displaystyle\left\|X^{\alpha,\leq M}_{s}\rho(X^{\alpha,\leq M}_{s})^{\dagger}-X^{\alpha,\leq M}_{s,\leq M}\rho(X^{\alpha,\leq M}_{s,\leq M})^{\dagger}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C_{f}\,q^{\prime}(M)\left(\left\|\left(X^{\alpha,\leq M}_{s}-X^{\alpha,\leq M}_{s,\leq M}\right)\rho\right\|_{1}+\left\|\rho\left((X^{\alpha,\leq M}_{s})^{\dagger}-(X^{\alpha,\leq M}_{s,\leq M})^{\dagger}\right)\right\|_{1}\right).

We focus on the term as the second term can be estimated the same way:
Using (
4.10
)
and (
4.32
) we see

\displaystyle\left\|\left(X^{\alpha,\leq M}_{s}-X^{\alpha,\leq M}_{s,\leq M}\right)\rho\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\int|f(t)|\Big(\left\|\left(e^{i(t+s)H}-e^{i(t+s)H_{\leq M}}\right)(A^{\alpha})^{\leq M}e^{-i(t+s)H}\rho\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left\|e^{i(t+s)H_{\leq M}}(A^{\alpha})^{\leq M}\left(e^{-i(t+s)H}-e^{-i(t+s)H_{\leq M}}\right)\rho\right\|_{1}\Big)dt

\displaystyle\leq 2q^{\prime}(M)\sqrt{E_{2}}\,p(|\mathcal{A}|,M)e^{-M^{\kappa}}\int|f(t)||t+s|e^{r|t+s|^{2\kappa}}dt

\displaystyle\leq 2\max\{C_{f},C^{\prime}_{f}\}\sqrt{E_{2}}\,q^{\prime}(M)p(|\mathcal{A}|,M)e^{-M^{\kappa}}(1+|s|)e^{r|s|^{2\kappa}}.

Using this and denoting the map

(4.37)

\displaystyle\Phi^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}(\rho):=\sigma_{E}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-2\sigma_{E}^{2}s^{2}}X^{\alpha,\leq M}_{s,\leq M}\rho(X^{\alpha,\leq M}_{s,\leq M})^{\dagger}ds,

we see that

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\Phi^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}-\Phi^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}\right)(\rho)\right\|_{1}\lesssim\,\widetilde{C}_{f}\,\sqrt{E_{2}}|\mathcal{A}|(q^{\prime}(M))^{2}p(|\mathcal{A}|,M)e^{-M^{\kappa}}\exp\left(r+\tfrac{(r+1)^{2}}{8\sigma^{2}_{E}}\right),

where we used that

\displaystyle\,\sigma_{E}\int e^{-2\sigma^{2}_{E}s^{2}}(1+|s|)e^{r|s|^{2\kappa}}ds

\displaystyle\leq e^{r}\,\sigma_{E}\int e^{-2\sigma^{2}_{E}s^{2}}(1+|s|)e^{r|s|}ds

\displaystyle\lesssim e^{r}\,\sigma_{E}\int e^{-2\sigma^{2}_{E}s^{2}}e^{(1+r)|s|}ds\lesssim\exp\left(r+\tfrac{(r+1)^{2}}{8\sigma^{2}_{E}}\right)

and where we only hide constants independent of all parameters with the
\lesssim
-notation.

∎

4.3.3.
Finite-dimensional Lindblad dynamics for
integrable filter functions

In this section we combine the results of the previous two sections, in particular Propositions
4.8
and
4.11
, to see in Theorem
4.12
that the dynamics
e^{t\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}}

for integrable filters satisfying the weighted time-moment hypothesis in Theorem
4.12
is
well approximated by
e^{t\mathcal{L}^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}}.

Moreover, under the assumptions of a simple kernel and a positive spectral gap of the corresponding unbounded generator on the Hilbert-Schmidt space, we then apply Theorem
4.12
to show efficient finite-dimensional preparation of the Gibbs state of
H
in Corollary
4.13
.

We first state both results and then give their proofs at the end of the section.

Theorem 4.12

(Finite-dimensional approximation of
e^{t\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}}
)
.

Let
f\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R})
and set of bare jumps
\{A^{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}
and assume
Condition

A
is satisfied.
Let further
N_{\mathcal{A}}
self-adjoint and positive semidefinite and either
0<\kappa<1/2
,
r\geq 0
, or
0<\kappa\leq 1
,
r=0
, and assume that (
4.10
), (
4.11
), (
4.12
) are satisfied and that
C_{A}:=\max_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\left(\|e^{N_{\mathcal{A}}^{\kappa}}A^{\alpha}e^{-2N_{\mathcal{A}}^{\kappa}}\|+\|A^{\alpha}e^{-N_{\mathcal{A}}^{\kappa}}\|+\|e^{-N_{\mathcal{A}}^{\kappa}}A^{\alpha}\|\right)+2Q<\infty
,
Q:=\sup_{M\geq 1}q(M)e^{-M^{\kappa}}<\infty
. Furthermore, assume that for
\kappa
as above (
4.28
) holds and, for this
r
, the condition (
4.13
) is satisfied and that
\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|t|e^{r|t|^{2\kappa}}|f(t)|dt<\infty.

For
\beta>0
we assume that the Gibbs state of
H,

\sigma_{\beta},
satisfies

(4.38)

\displaystyle E_{\operatorname{Gibbs}}:=\operatorname{Tr}\left(e^{4N_{\mathcal{A}}^{\kappa}}\,\sigma_{\beta}\right)<\infty

and consider state
\rho
such that

(4.39)

\displaystyle\rho\leq\mathfrak{c}\,\sigma_{\beta}

for some
\mathfrak{c}\geq 1.
Then for all
\sigma_{E}\in(0,\infty),

t\geq 0
and
M\in\mathbb{N}
we have

(4.40)

\displaystyle\left\|\left(e^{t\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}}-e^{t\mathcal{L}^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}}\right)(\rho)\right\|_{1}\leq t\,C_{1}(\beta,r,\kappa,\sigma_{E},f,C_{A})\,\mathfrak{c}\,p_{4}(|\mathcal{A}|,M)E_{\operatorname{Gibbs}}\,e^{-M^{\kappa}},

where
C_{1}(\beta,r,\kappa,\sigma_{E},f,C_{A})\geq 0
is some constant depending only on the displayed parameters and
p_{4}(|\mathcal{A}|,M)
is some polynomially bounded function. Therefore, for
\varepsilon>0,
we can achieve

(4.41)

\displaystyle\left\|\left(e^{t\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}}-e^{t\mathcal{L}^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}}\right)(\rho)\right\|_{1}\leq\varepsilon,\qquad\text{with}\qquad M=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\left(\log\left(2+\frac{t\,\mathfrak{c}\,E_{\operatorname{Gibbs}}|\mathcal{A}|}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)^{1/\kappa}\right),

where the
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}
notation hides constants independent of the displayed parameters and additionally suppresses subdominant
\operatorname{poly}\log\log
factors.

Corollary 4.13

(Gibbs state preparation)
.

Under the same assumptions as in Theorem
4.12
and assuming additionally that
L_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H},
the self-adjoint generator on
\mathscr{T}_{2}(\mathcal{H})
associated to the Lindbladian
\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H},
has a positive spectral gap

\lambda_{2}\equiv\operatorname{gap}\left(L_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}\right)>0
and
\ker L_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}=\mathbb{C}\sqrt{\sigma_{\beta}}
,
we can achieve for all
0<\varepsilon<1

\displaystyle\left\|e^{t\mathcal{L}^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}}(\rho)-\sigma_{\beta}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon,

\displaystyle t

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\lambda_{2}}\log\left(2+\frac{\mathfrak{c}}{\varepsilon}\right)\right),

\displaystyle M

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\left(\log\left(2+\frac{(1+\lambda_{2}^{-1})\mathfrak{c}\,E_{\operatorname{Gibbs}}|\mathcal{A}|}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)^{1/\kappa}\right),

where the
\mathcal{O}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}
notations hide constants independent of the displayed parameters and
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}
additionally suppresses subdominant
\operatorname{poly}\log\log
factors.

Proof of Theorem
4.12
.

We use that

\displaystyle\left(e^{t\mathcal{L}^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}}-e^{t\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}}\right)(\rho)=\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-s)\mathcal{L}^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}}\left(\mathcal{L}^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}-\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}\right)e^{s\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}}(\rho)ds

and therefore, denoting
\rho(s):=e^{s\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}}(\rho),
using Proposition
4.8
and
4.11
we see

\displaystyle\left\|\left(e^{t\mathcal{L}^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}}-e^{t\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}}\right)(\rho)\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq t\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\left\|\left(\mathcal{L}^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}-\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}\right)(\rho(s))\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq t\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\left\|\left(\mathcal{L}^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}-\mathcal{L}^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}\right)(\rho(s))\right\|_{1}+t\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\left\|\left(\mathcal{L}^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}-\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}\right)(\rho(s))\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq t\,C_{1}(\beta,r,\kappa,\sigma_{E},f,C_{A})p_{4}(|\mathcal{A}|,M)e^{-M^{\kappa}}\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\max\left\{\sqrt{\operatorname{Tr}\left(e^{4N_{\mathcal{A}}^{\kappa}}\rho(s)\right)}\,,\,\operatorname{Tr}\left(e^{2N_{\mathcal{A}}^{\kappa}}\rho(s)\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq t\,\mathfrak{c}\,C_{1}(\beta,r,\kappa,\sigma_{E},f,C_{A})p_{4}(|\mathcal{A}|,M)e^{-M^{\kappa}}E_{\operatorname{Gibbs}},

where
C_{1}(\beta,r,\kappa,\sigma_{E},f,C_{A})\geq 0
is some constant depending only on the displayed parameters,
p_{4}(|\mathcal{A}|,M)
is some polynomially bounded function and where we used in the last inequality that by (
4.39
), positivity of the map
e^{s\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}}
and the fact that
\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}
is KMS-symmetric we have

\displaystyle\rho(s)\leq\mathfrak{c}\,e^{s\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}}(\sigma_{\beta})=\mathfrak{c}\,\sigma_{\beta}.

∎

Proof of Corollary
4.13
.

We split

(4.42)

\displaystyle\left\|e^{t\mathcal{L}^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}}(\rho)-\sigma_{\beta}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|e^{t\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}}(\rho)-\sigma_{\beta}\right\|_{1}+\left\|\left(e^{t\mathcal{L}^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}}-e^{t\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}}\right)(\rho)\right\|_{1}

For the first term, we argue as in Section
1.2
using positivity of spectral gap of
L_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}
together with (
4.39
) to get

\displaystyle\left\|e^{t\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}}(\rho)-\sigma_{\beta}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\lambda_{2}t}\left\|\sigma_{\beta}^{-1/4}\rho\sigma^{-1/4}_{\beta}\right\|_{2}=e^{-\lambda_{2}t}\sqrt{\operatorname{Tr}\left(\sigma^{-1/2}_{\beta}\rho\sigma^{-1/2}_{\beta}\rho\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mathfrak{c}}\,e^{-\lambda_{2}t}\sqrt{\operatorname{Tr}(\rho)}=\sqrt{\mathfrak{c}}\,e^{-\lambda_{2}t},

which gives the desired bound on the mixing time
t.
The result then follows by Theorem
4.12
.
∎

The warm-start hypothesis
\rho\leq\mathfrak{c}\,\sigma_{\beta}
for some
\mathfrak{c}\geq 1
in (
4.39
) can alternatively be replaced by

R:=\|\sigma_{\beta}^{-1/4}\rho\sigma_{\beta}^{-1/4}\|_{2}<\infty,\qquad E_{8}:=\operatorname{Tr}(e^{8N_{\mathcal{A}}^{\kappa}}\sigma_{\beta})<\infty.

Writing
x_{s}=e^{sL_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}}(\sigma_{\beta}^{-1/4}\rho\sigma_{\beta}^{-1/4})
, contractivity gives

\|x_{s}\|_{2}\leq R
. For bounded spectral truncations
B
of

e^{4N_{\mathcal{A}}^{\kappa}}
, Schatten Cauchy–Schwarz gives

\operatorname{Tr}(B\rho(s))\leq R\|\sigma_{\beta}^{1/4}B\sigma_{\beta}^{1/4}\|_{2}\leq R\,[\operatorname{Tr}(\sigma_{\beta}B^{2})]^{1/2}.

The last inequality follows by applying Hilbert–Schmidt Cauchy–Schwarz
to
B\sigma_{\beta}^{1/2}
and its adjoint. Monotone convergence therefore
bounds
\operatorname{Tr}(e^{4N_{\mathcal{A}}^{\kappa}}\rho(s))
by
RE_{8}^{1/2}
.
Moreover, normalization gives

\operatorname{Tr}(e^{2N_{\mathcal{A}}^{\kappa}}\rho(s))^{2}\leq\operatorname{Tr}(e^{4N_{\mathcal{A}}^{\kappa}}\rho(s))
.
The proof of Theorem
4.12
thus yields

\|(e^{t\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}}-e^{t\mathcal{L}^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}})(\rho)\|_{1}\leq tC_{1}p_{4}(|\mathcal{A}|,M)e^{-M^{\kappa}}R^{1/2}E_{8}^{1/4}.

For the original warm-start branch the same argument sharpens its
prefactor
\mathfrak{c}E_{\operatorname{Gibbs}}
to

\sqrt{\mathfrak{c}E_{\operatorname{Gibbs}}}
.
The generator-domain requirements in the Duhamel argument hold because,
for every
p\geq 0
, the same estimate gives

\operatorname{Tr}(\widetilde{H}^{p}\rho(s))\leq R[\operatorname{Tr}(\widetilde{H}^{2p}\sigma_{\beta})]^{1/2}<\infty
.
With a simple kernel and gap
\lambda_{2}
, the mixing estimate is

\|e^{t\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}}(\rho)-\sigma_{\beta}\|_{1}\leq\sqrt{R^{2}-1}\,e^{-\lambda_{2}t};

in the warm-start branch it is at most

\sqrt{\mathfrak{c}-1}\,e^{-\lambda_{2}t}
.
All subsequent circuit bounds also apply to these inputs, with

\mathfrak{c}E_{\operatorname{Gibbs}}
in the resource logarithms
replaced by
RE_{8}
, provided the normalized finite-register preparation
oracle is supplied. The hypothesis on
E_{8}
is stronger than the original
fourth-moment hypothesis, which remains sufficient for warm starts.
This enlarges the input class: for
H=N
and
0<\kappa<1
, a thermal
input at inverse temperature
\beta/2<\beta^{\prime}<\beta
has finite
R
but
is not bounded above by any finite multiple of
\sigma_{\beta}
.

4.4.
Finite-dimensional circuit implementation

In this final implementation section we show how the dynamics resulting from the generator
\mathcal{L}_{\smash{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}}^{\leq M}
can be implemented by a quantum circuit through discretizations of the integral representations of its jump and coherent parts. By the integral representation in Proposition
4.4
, we note that the generator
\mathcal{L}_{\smash{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}}^{\leq M}
is constructed from a continuous family of jump operators. In Section
4.4.1
below we, therefore, first establish that this generator can be written as a Gaussian integral over certain generators of the form (
1.3
) which can then be approximated by a Lindblad generator with a finite number of jumps using Gauss-Hermite quadratures, c.f. Proposition
4.17
. After this discretization, and assuming the filter function satisfies
[
6
, Assumption 13]
, as recalled in
Condition

4.15
and
4.18
, we can invoke
[
6
, Theorem 18]
to obtain an efficient circuit implementation of the finite-dimensional Lindblad dynamics.

Finally, combining this with
Theorem
4.12
yields an efficient
finite-dimensional circuit implementation of

e^{t\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}}
for integrable filters satisfying
the stated weighted-moment and Gevrey assumptions, in
Theorem
4.23
. Given a simple kernel and a positive spectral gap
of the associated generators on the space of Hilbert-Schmidt operators,
Corollary
4.25
then gives
efficient Gibbs state preparation. We verify below that the
polynomial filters
\widehat{f}_{\eta}
,
\eta>0
, satisfy the filter
assumptions directly.

4.4.1.
Integral discretizations

We start by recalling
[
6
, Assumption 13]
for the cut-off part of the filter function.

Definition 4.14

(Gevrey function)
.

A complex-valued
C^{\infty}
function

h:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{C}
is called a
Gevrey function of order

s\geq 0
if there exist
constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0
such that, for every nonnegative integer

n
, the derivatives of
h
satisfy

(4.43)

\|h^{(n)}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\leq C_{1}C_{2}^{n}n^{ns}.

For fixed constants
C_{1},C_{2},s
, the set of such Gevrey functions is denoted by
\mathcal{G}^{s}_{C_{1},C_{2}}
. Furthermore, we define

\mathcal{G}^{s}=\bigcup_{C_{1},C_{2}>0}\mathcal{G}^{s}_{C_{1},C_{2}}.

Condition 4.15
.

In what follows, we also consider a cut-off function
w\in\mathcal{G}^{s}_{\xi_{q},\xi_{w}}
for some
s>1
and
\xi_{w}\geq 1
, with
w(\nu)=\overline{w(-\nu)}
for all
\nu\in\mathbb{R}
and such that

w(\nu)=1\quad\text{when }|\nu|\leq\tfrac{1}{2},\qquad w(\nu)=0\quad\text{when }|\nu|\geq 1.

Finally, we denote by
\kappa(\nu)=w(\nu/S)
for some
S>0
which we will choose later.

Next, for
f\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R})
we show that the
generator
\mathcal{L}_{\smash{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}}^{\leq M}

from Section
4.3.2
can be approximated by a
Lindblad generator with a finite number of jumps , depending on
the requested accuracy and truncation parameters . We recall that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\smash{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}}^{\leq M}(\rho)=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\gamma(t)X_{t,\leq M}^{\alpha,\leq M}\rho\left(X_{t,\leq M}^{\alpha,\leq M}\right)^{\dagger}dt\,+G^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\leq M}\rho+\rho\left(G^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\leq M}\right)^{\dagger}

with

\displaystyle G^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\leq M}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}g(t)(X_{t,\leq M}^{\alpha,\leq M})^{\dagger}X_{t,\leq M}^{\alpha,\leq M}dt,\quad

\displaystyle X_{t,\leq M}^{\alpha,\leq M}:=e^{itH_{\leq M}}L_{\leq M}^{\alpha,\leq M}e^{-itH_{\leq M}},

\displaystyle\gamma(t)

\displaystyle:=\sigma_{E}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}e^{-2\sigma_{E}^{2}t^{2}}\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{and}

\displaystyle g(t):=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{e^{-\nu^{2}/8\sigma_{E}^{2}}}{1+e^{\beta\nu/2}}e^{-i\nu t}d\nu

and
L^{\alpha,\leq M}_{\leq M}
has been defined in (
4.30
).
By decomposing the function
g
into real and imaginary part as

\displaystyle g(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int\frac{e^{-\nu^{2}/8\sigma_{E}^{2}}}{1+e^{\beta\nu/2}}e^{-i\nu t}d\nu=\frac{1}{2}\gamma(t)+\frac{1}{2\pi}\int{e^{-\nu^{2}/8\sigma_{E}^{2}}}e^{-i\nu t}\frac{1-e^{\beta\nu/2}}{2(1+e^{\beta\nu/2})}d\nu

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\gamma(t)+\frac{1}{4\pi}\int{e^{-\nu^{2}/8\sigma_{E}^{2}}}e^{-i\nu t}\tanh(-\beta\nu/4)d\nu,

we get that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\smash{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}}^{\leq M}(\rho)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha}\int\gamma(t)\left(X_{t,\leq M}^{\alpha,\leq M}\rho\left(X_{t,\leq M}^{\alpha,\leq M}\right)^{\dagger}-\frac{1}{2}\left\{\left(X_{t,\leq M}^{\alpha,\leq M}\right)^{\dagger}X_{t,\leq M}^{\alpha,\leq M},\rho\right\}\right)dt-i\big[B^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E}},\rho\big],

with

\displaystyle B_{\sigma_{E}}^{\leq M}:=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{(-i)}{4\pi}\int\int{e^{-\nu^{2}/8\sigma_{E}^{2}}}e^{-i\nu t}\tanh(-\beta\nu/4)e^{itH_{\leq M}}(L_{\leq M}^{\alpha,\leq M})^{\dagger}L_{\leq M}^{\alpha,\leq M}e^{-itH_{\leq M}}\,d\nu dt=(B_{\sigma_{E}}^{\leq M})^{\dagger},

where the self-adjointness follows from the antisymmetry of
\tanh
. Decomposing into the eigenbasis of
H_{\leq M}=\sum_{E\in\operatorname{Sp}(H_{\leq M})}E\,P_{E}
and denoting
\big[(L_{\leq M}^{\alpha,\leq M})^{\dagger}L_{\leq M}^{\alpha,\leq M}\big]_{\mu}=\sum_{E^{\prime}-E=\mu}P_{E^{\prime}}(L_{\leq M}^{\alpha,\leq M})^{\dagger}L_{\leq M}^{\alpha,\leq M}P_{E}
, we get

\displaystyle B_{\sigma_{E}}^{\leq M}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sum_{\mu\in B(H_{\leq M})}\frac{(-i)}{4\pi}\int\int{e^{-\nu^{2}/8\sigma_{E}^{2}}}e^{-i(\nu-\mu)t}\tanh(-\beta\nu/4)\big[(L_{\leq M}^{\alpha,\leq M})^{\dagger}L_{\leq M}^{\alpha,\leq M}\big]_{\mu}\,d\nu dt

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sum_{\mu\in B(H_{\leq M})}\frac{(-i)}{2}{e^{-\mu^{2}/8\sigma_{E}^{2}}}\tanh(-\beta\mu/4)\big[(L_{\leq M}^{\alpha,\leq M})^{\dagger}L_{\leq M}^{\alpha,\leq M}\big]_{\mu}.

Using the notation from
Condition

4.15
, choose
S\geq 4\|H_{\leq M}\|
and define the Schwartz function

\widehat{t}_{\kappa}(\mu):=-\frac{i}{2}\tanh(-\beta\mu/4)\kappa(\mu).

Every Bohr frequency
\mu\in B(H_{\leq M})
satisfies

|\mu|\leq 2\|H_{\leq M}\|\leq S/2
, so

\widehat{t}_{\kappa}(\mu)=-\frac{i}{2}\tanh(-\beta\mu/4)
. Hence

\displaystyle B_{\sigma_{E}}^{\leq M}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{E}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sum_{\mu\in B(H_{\leq M})}\int e^{-i\mu t-2\sigma_{E}^{2}t^{2}}\widehat{t}_{\kappa}(\mu)\big[(L_{\leq M}^{\alpha,\leq M})^{\dagger}L_{\leq M}^{\alpha,\leq M}\big]_{\mu}dt

\displaystyle=\sigma_{E}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sum_{\mu\in B(H_{\leq M})}\int e^{-2\sigma_{E}^{2}t^{2}}\widehat{t}_{\kappa}(\mu)e^{-itH_{\leq M}}\big[(L_{\leq M}^{\alpha,\leq M})^{\dagger}L_{\leq M}^{\alpha,\leq M}\big]_{\mu}e^{itH_{\leq M}}\,dt

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\int\gamma(t){t}_{\kappa}(s)e^{i(s-t)H_{\leq M}}(L_{\leq M}^{\alpha,\leq M})^{\dagger}L_{\leq M}^{\alpha,\leq M}e^{-i(s-t)H_{\leq M}}\,ds\,dt

\displaystyle=\int\gamma(t)\,e^{-itH_{\leq M}}B^{\leq M}e^{itH_{\leq M}}dt,

where in the last equality we have denoted

\displaystyle B^{\leq M}:=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}t_{\kappa}(s)\,e^{isH_{\leq M}}\left(L^{\alpha,\leq M}_{\leq M}\right)^{\dagger}L^{\alpha,\leq M}_{\leq M}e^{-isH_{\leq M}}\,ds.

Evenness of
\gamma
makes the two time conventions equivalent.
All in all, we have found that

(4.44)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}^{\leq M}(\rho)=\int\gamma(t)\,\mathcal{L}_{\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}^{\leq M,\,t}(\rho)\,dt,

where
\mathcal{L}_{\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}^{\leq M,\,t}
coincides with the generator
\mathcal{L}_{\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}
defined in (
1.3
) with filter function
\widehat{f}
, jumps
\left(A^{\alpha}\right)^{\leq M}_{t}:=e^{itH_{\leq M}}(A^{\alpha})^{\leq M}e^{-itH_{\leq M}}
and Hamiltonian
H_{\leq M}
.

Next, we need to argue that, without loss of generality, the filter function
\widehat{f}
can be replaced by
\widehat{f}_{\kappa}(\nu):=\widehat{f}(\nu)\kappa(\nu)
with
\kappa
satisfying
Condition

4.15
for some choice of parameter
S
. This is done by observing that

\displaystyle L_{\leq M}^{\alpha,\leq M}=\int f(s)\,e^{isH_{\leq M}}(A^{\alpha})^{\leq M}e^{-isH_{\leq M}}\,ds

\displaystyle=\sum_{\nu\in B(H_{\leq M})}\widehat{f}(\nu)((A^{\alpha})^{\leq M})_{\nu}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\nu\in B(H_{\leq M})}\widehat{f}_{\kappa}(\nu)((A^{\alpha})^{\leq M})_{\nu}

(4.45)

\displaystyle=\int f_{\kappa}(s)\,e^{isH_{\leq M}}(A^{\alpha})^{\leq M}e^{-isH_{\leq M}}ds,

for
S\geq 4\|H_{\leq M}\|
.
Indeed, if
k_{S}
is the inverse Fourier
transform of
\kappa
, then
k_{S}
is Schwartz and

f_{\kappa}=f*k_{S}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R})
, by the convolution theorem
and Young’s inequality. Thus the integral identities remain
valid for any
f\in L^{1}
, even if it has an integrable singularity
at the origin. The cutoff does not change any Bohr-frequency
coefficient of the finite-dimensional generator.
Thus, without loss of generality, we can
replace
\mathcal{L}_{\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}^{\leq M,\,t}
by
\mathcal{L}_{\widehat{f}_{\kappa},H_{\leq M}}^{\leq M,\,t}
in
Equation

4.44
.

We invoke Gaussian-Hermite quadratures, recalled in the following supporting lemma, to find a discretization of the Gaussian integral in (
4.44
) in Proposition
4.17
below.

Lemma 4.16
.

Let
X
be a Banach space,
F:\mathbb{R}\to X
be
C^{\infty}
and assume that

\|F^{(m)}(t)\|_{X}\leq K\,C^{m}\qquad\text{for all }m\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\ t\in\mathbb{R},

for some constants
C,\,K>0
. Let further
\sigma_{E},\varepsilon>0.
Let
\{(x_{k},w_{k})\}_{k=1}^{n}\subset\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}
be the
n
-point Gauss–Hermite nodes and weights with
\max_{k}|x_{k}|\leq 2\sqrt{n}
and
\max_{k}w_{k}=\mathcal{O}(n^{-1/2})
, and define

Q_{n}(F):=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}w_{k}\,F\!\left(\frac{x_{k}}{\sqrt{2}\,\sigma_{E}}\right).

Then,

n\geq\max\left\{1,\frac{C^{2}}{2\sigma_{E}^{2}},\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{K}{\varepsilon}\right)\right\}\quad\Rightarrow\quad\left\|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\gamma(t)\,F(t)\,dt-Q_{n}(F)\right\|_{X}\leq\varepsilon.

Proof.

If
X
is complex, regard it as a real Banach space with the same norm.
For any bounded real-linear functional
\varphi:X\to\mathbb{R}
with

\|\varphi\|\leq 1
, apply the scalar Gauss–Hermite remainder formula

[
42
, §3.5(v), equations (3.5.19) and (3.5.28)]

to the real-valued function
g_{\varphi}(x)=\varphi(F(x/(\sqrt{2}\sigma_{E})))
.
Its derivatives satisfy

|g_{\varphi}^{(2n)}(x)|\leq K(C/(\sqrt{2}\sigma_{E}))^{2n}
.
Bounded real-linear functionals commute with the integral and the
quadrature sum. Taking the supremum over these functionals and using
the real Hahn–Banach theorem therefore gives

\left\|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\gamma(t)F(t)\,dt-Q_{n}(F)\right\|_{X}\leq K\frac{n!}{(2n)!}\left(\frac{C}{2\sigma_{E}}\right)^{2n}\leq K\left(\frac{C^{2}}{4\sigma_{E}^{2}n}\right)^{n}.

The stated conditions on
n
make the last expression at most

K2^{-n}\leq\varepsilon
.
∎

Proposition 4.17
.

Let
H
be such that the truncated Hamiltonian
H_{\leq M}
, defined in Section
4.3.2
, satisfies
\|H_{\leq M}\|\leq p_{5}(|\mathcal{A}|,M)
for some polynomially bounded function
p_{5}.
Moreover, let
\{A^{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}
be a set of bare jumps satisfying (
4.10
), i.e.
\|\left(A^{\alpha}\right)^{\leq M}\|\leq q^{\prime}(M)
for some polynomially bounded function
q^{\prime}(M).

Let
\kappa
satisfy
Condition

4.15
for
s>1
and
\xi_{q},\xi_{w}\geq 0
and

S=\max\{1,4p_{5}(|\mathcal{A}|,M)\}.

Let furthermore
\beta>0
and

f\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R})
and denote

\displaystyle K:=|\mathcal{A}|\xi_{q}\,\bigl(1+\log\left(2+(\beta+\xi_{w}/S)\max\{S,1\}\right)\bigr)\,\left(q^{\prime}(M)\right)^{2}\min\left\{\|f\|^{2}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R})},\|f_{\kappa}\|^{2}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R})}\right\}.

Then for
\beta,\varepsilon>0
and
t\geq 0
we can achieve

(4.46)

\displaystyle\Big\|e^{t\mathcal{L}^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}}-e^{t\mathcal{L}^{\leq M,\mathfrak{n}}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}}\Big\|_{1\to 1}\leq\varepsilon\qquad\text{with}\qquad\mathfrak{n}=\mathcal{O}\left(1+\frac{S^{2}}{\sigma_{E}^{2}}+\log\Big(2+\frac{Kt}{\varepsilon}\Big)\right)

with, given the
\mathfrak{n}
-point Gauss-Hermite nodes and weights
\{(x_{k},w_{k})\}_{k=1}^{\mathfrak{n}}
with
\max_{k}w_{k}=\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{n}^{-1/2})
and
\max_{k}|x_{k}|\leq 2\mathfrak{n}^{1/2}
,

(4.47)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\leq M,\mathfrak{n}}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi}}\sum_{k=1}^{\mathfrak{n}}w_{k}\,\mathcal{L}_{\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}^{\leq M,x_{k}/(\sqrt{2}\sigma_{E})}

Proof.

We use

\displaystyle e^{t\mathcal{L}^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}}-e^{t\mathcal{L}^{\leq M,\mathfrak{n}}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}}=\int^{t}_{0}e^{s\mathcal{L}^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}}\left(\mathcal{L}^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}-\mathcal{L}^{\leq M,\mathfrak{n}}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}\right)e^{(t-s)\mathcal{L}^{\leq M,\mathfrak{n}}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}}\,ds

and, therefore, as both dynamics are contractive in trace norm and the
1\to 1
norm is submultiplicative, we have

\displaystyle\Big\|e^{t\mathcal{L}^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}}-e^{t\mathcal{L}^{\leq M,\mathfrak{n}}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}}\Big\|_{1\to 1}\leq t\left\|\mathcal{L}^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}-\mathcal{L}^{\leq M,\mathfrak{n}}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}\right\|_{1\to 1}

Hence, using Lemma
4.16
for the function
t\mapsto F(t):=\mathcal{L}_{\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}^{\leq M,t}
, it suffices to compute estimate the derivatives as

\displaystyle\|F^{(m)}(t)\|_{1\to 1}

\displaystyle\leq 2|\mathcal{A}|\,(4\|H_{\leq M}\|)^{m}\max_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\left\|L_{\leq M}^{\alpha,\leq M}\right\|^{2}+2(4\|H_{\leq M}\|)^{m}\|B^{\leq M}\|

\displaystyle\leq 2|\mathcal{A}|\,(4\|H_{\leq M}\|)^{m}\max_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\left\|L_{\leq M}^{\alpha,\leq M}\right\|^{2}(1+\|t_{\kappa}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R})})

\displaystyle\leq 2|\mathcal{A}|\,(4\|H_{\leq M}\|)^{m}\min\left\{\|f\|^{2}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R})},\|f_{\kappa}\|^{2}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R})}\right\}\max_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\left\|\left(A^{\alpha}\right)^{\leq M}\right\|^{2}(1+\|t_{\kappa}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R})})

\displaystyle\leq 2|\mathcal{A}|\,(4\|H_{\leq M}\|)^{m}\min\left\{\|f\|^{2}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R})},\|f_{\kappa}\|^{2}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R})}\right\}(q^{\prime}(M))^{2}(1+\|t_{\kappa}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R})}),

where in the second to last inequality we have used (
4.45
) to bound the operator norm of
L_{\leq M}^{\alpha,\leq M}.

Using now
Condition

4.15
combined with
[
6
, Lemma 30]
, we see

\displaystyle\|t_{\kappa}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R})}=\mathcal{O}\bigl(\xi_{q}\,(1+\log(2+(\beta+\xi_{w}/S)\max\{S,1\}))\bigr),

which finishes the proof.
∎

We can now employ the
[
6
, Theorem 18]
to provide a circuit implementation of the finite-dimensional dynamics. For that we first recall the condition on the filter function in
[
6
, Assumption 13]
.

Condition 4.18
.

For
\beta>0
, we consider a filter function
\widehat{f}
such that
\nu\mapsto\widehat{f}(\nu/\beta)\in\mathcal{G}^{s}_{\smash{\xi_{q},\xi_{u}}}
for some constants
\xi_{q},\xi_{u}\geq 1
and
s\geq 1
. In addition, we assume

\frac{d}{d\nu}\widehat{f}(\nu)\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}),

and denote

C_{1,f}:=\left\|\frac{d}{d\nu}\widehat{f}(\nu/\beta)\right\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R})}.

Together with the KMS symmetry in
(
2.2
), this is the filter part of

[
6
, Assumption 13]
: set

u(x)=e^{x/4}\widehat{f}(x/\beta)
, so that

u(-x)=\overline{u(x)}
, and use the window from
Condition
4.15
. The corresponding weighting function is

u(\beta\nu)\kappa(\nu)
, whose product with

e^{-\beta\nu/4}
is precisely
\widehat{f}_{\kappa}(\nu)
.
Under these assumptions
\widehat{f}_{\kappa}
is smooth with
compact support, so
f_{\kappa}
is Schwartz even when
f
is not.

The polynomial filters satisfy the required Gevrey
condition directly.

Lemma 4.19
.

For every
\eta>0
and
\beta>0
, the filter
\widehat{f}_{\eta}

in (
3.15
) satisfies
Condition
4.18
.
More precisely, with
h_{\eta}(x):=\widehat{f}_{\eta}(x/\beta)
and

C_{\eta}=(4/3)^{\eta/2}
,

\|h_{\eta}^{(n)}\|_{\infty}\leq C_{\eta}\,4^{n}n!,\qquad n\geq 0,\qquad\|h_{\eta}^{\prime}\|_{1}\leq 3.

Thus one can take
\xi_{q}=\max\{1,C_{\eta}\}
,
\xi_{u}=4
and

s=1
(and hence any
s>1
).

Proof.

The function

h_{\eta}(z)=\frac{e^{-(\sqrt{1+z^{2}}+z)/4}}{(1+z^{2})^{\eta/2}}

is holomorphic on
|\Im z|<1
, using the principal branches.
For
z=x+iy
with
|y|\leq 1/2
, one has

|1+z^{2}|\geq\Re(1+z^{2})\geq 3/4
and

\Re\sqrt{1+z^{2}}\geq|x|
. Hence
|h_{\eta}(z)|\leq C_{\eta}
.
Cauchy’s estimate on the circle
|z-x|=1/4
gives the displayed
derivative bound; use
n!\leq n^{n}
for
n\geq 1
.
For the
L^{1}
bound, write
h_{\eta}=uv
on the real axis, where

u(x)=e^{-(\sqrt{1+x^{2}}+x)/4}
and

v(x)=(1+x^{2})^{-\eta/2}
. Then
0<u,v\leq 1
,

u
decreases from
1
to
0
, and
v
increases from
0
to
1

on
(-\infty,0)
and decreases back to
0
on
(0,\infty)
.
Consequently,

\|h_{\eta}^{\prime}\|_{1}\leq\|u^{\prime}\|_{1}+\|v^{\prime}\|_{1}=1+2=3.

These bounds are independent of
\beta
.
∎

We next reduce the quadrature generator (
4.47
) to the
setting of
[
6
, Theorem 18]
. The reduction keeps track of
the jump normalization, the Gauss–Hermite weights, the additional Hamiltonian
conjugations, and the required oracle access, leading to the following circuit
complexity bound.

Corollary 4.20

(Circuit simulation of the quadrature generator)
.

Let
\beta,\sigma_{E}>0
,
M,\mathfrak{n}\in\mathbb{N}
,
t\geq 0
, and

0<\varepsilon<1
. Consider the invariant finite-dimensional system register

\operatorname{im}(P_{M})
from Section
4.3.2
and assume
the adjoint-pair convention, the norm bound (
4.10
)
with
q^{\prime}(M)\geq 1
, the KMS symmetry (
2.2
), and
Conditions
4.18
and
4.15
with a common order

s>1
and

S\geq\max\{1,4\|H_{\leq M}\|\}.

Define

\kappa_{B}(\nu):=w(\nu/(4S)),\qquad\widehat{t}_{\kappa_{B}}(\nu):=-\frac{i}{2}\tanh(-\beta\nu/4)\kappa_{B}(\nu).

Assume coherent, index-controlled access to block encodings of

(A^{\alpha})^{\leq M}/q^{\prime}(M)
and their adjoints, controlled Hamiltonian
simulation with respect to
H_{\leq M}
, and the state-preparation oracles
for
f_{\kappa}
and
t_{\kappa_{B}}
required in

[
6
, Eqs. (3.27)-(3.31)]
. Assume further coherent
reversible access to the Gauss-Hermite nodes and weights to the precision
required by the simulation.

Then the restriction of

e^{t\mathcal{L}^{\leq M,\mathfrak{n}}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}}

to the system register can be simulated within
\varepsilon
in diamond norm
with total Hamiltonian simulation time

(4.48)

\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left([1+t(q^{\prime}(M))^{2}]\mathfrak{n}^{2}|\mathcal{A}|^{2}\left(\beta+1+\frac{\sqrt{\mathfrak{n}}}{\sigma_{E}}\right)[1+\log^{2+s}S]\log^{1+s}(2/\varepsilon)\right).

In addition to the system register and the workspace required by the
supplied oracles, the number of ancillary qubits is at most

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\Bigg(

\displaystyle\log(2+S^{2}+S\|H_{\leq M}\|)+\log^{2}\left(2+\frac{[1+t(q^{\prime}(M))^{2}]\mathfrak{n}|\mathcal{A}|}{\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle+\log(2+\beta)+\log\left(2+\frac{\|H_{\leq M}\|\sqrt{\mathfrak{n}}}{\sigma_{E}}\right)\Bigg).

The hidden constants depend only on

s,\xi_{q},\xi_{u},\xi_{w},C_{1,f}
, and

\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}
additionally suppresses subdominant
polylogarithmic factors in the displayed parameters.

Proof.

Write
H_{M}:=H_{\leq M}
. We first reduce the quadrature generator to the
normalised setting of
[
6
, Theorem 18]
. For each
non-self-adjoint pair
A,A^{\dagger}
, replace the two jumps by

(A+A^{\dagger})/\sqrt{2}
and
(A-A^{\dagger})/(i\sqrt{2})
, leaving
self-adjoint jumps unchanged. This unitary change of jump labels leaves
the completely positive part,
\sum_{\alpha}(L^{\alpha})^{\dagger}L^{\alpha}
,
and hence also the coherent correction unchanged. Denoting the resulting
self-adjoint jumps by
A_{j}^{\mathrm{sa}}
, we have

\|A_{j}^{\mathrm{sa}}\|\leq\sqrt{2}\,q^{\prime}(M)
.

Let
\{(x_{k},w_{k})\}_{k=1}^{\mathfrak{n}}
be the Gauss-Hermite nodes and
weights from Proposition
4.17
, and set

p_{k}:=\frac{w_{k}}{\sqrt{\pi}},\qquad s_{k}:=\frac{x_{k}}{\sqrt{2}\sigma_{E}},\qquad R:=\sqrt{2}\,q^{\prime}(M).

Since Gauss-Hermite quadrature is exact on constants,
p_{k}>0
and

\sum_{k}p_{k}=1
. We define the self-adjoint contractions

\overline{A}^{j,k}:=\frac{\sqrt{p_{k}}}{R}e^{is_{k}H_{M}}A_{j}^{\mathrm{sa}}e^{-is_{k}H_{M}}.

There are at most
N=\mathfrak{n}|\mathcal{A}|
such jumps. Since filtering
commutes with Hamiltonian conjugation, the corresponding filtered jumps
are

\overline{L}_{j,k}=\frac{\sqrt{p_{k}}}{R}e^{is_{k}H_{M}}L_{j}e^{-is_{k}H_{M}}.

Moreover, the choices of
\kappa
and
\kappa_{B}
leave all frequencies
entering the finite-dimensional jump and coherent terms unchanged.
Therefore, the generator
\overline{\mathcal{L}}
associated with the
jumps
\overline{A}^{j,k}
in the framework of

[
6
, Theorem 18]
satisfies

(4.49)

\overline{\mathcal{L}}=R^{-2}\mathcal{L}^{\leq M,\mathfrak{n}}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{M}}.

It is consequently enough to simulate
\overline{\mathcal{L}}
for time

t^{\prime}=R^{2}t=2(q^{\prime}(M))^{2}t
.

The KMS symmetry together with Conditions
4.18
and

4.15
gives precisely the filter assumptions of

[
6
, Theorem 18]
. In particular, with

u(x):=e^{x/4}\widehat{f}(x/\beta)
, the KMS symmetry gives

u(-x)=\overline{u(x)}
, while the coherent filter

t_{\kappa_{B}}
is the enlarged-window filter prescribed in

[
6
, Eq. (3.17)]
.

It remains to account for the additional Hamiltonian conjugations.
A block encoding of
\overline{A}^{j,k}
is obtained from that of

A_{j}^{\mathrm{sa}}/R
by a controlled rotation of amplitude

\sqrt{p_{k}}
and the conjugations
e^{\pm is_{k}H_{M}}
. Since

\max_{k}|x_{k}|\leq 2\sqrt{\mathfrak{n}}
,

\max_{k}|s_{k}|=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\mathfrak{n}}}{\sigma_{E}}\right).

Hence each such query requires at most

\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{\mathfrak{n}}/\sigma_{E})
additional Hamiltonian
simulation time. Applying
[
6
, Theorem 18]
with

N=\mathfrak{n}|\mathcal{A}|
and
t^{\prime}=2(q^{\prime}(M))^{2}t
now gives
(
4.48
).

The ancillary-qubit estimate follows from the corresponding estimate in

[
6
, Theorem 18]
, together with the registers used
for the quadrature label and the reversible evaluation of the nodes and
weights. Choosing the precision of these auxiliary operations below the
corresponding fraction of the total error budget and applying the
standard hybrid argument yields total diamond-norm error at most
\varepsilon
.
∎

Remark 4.21

(Circuit implementation of
a_{i}^{\leq M}
and
(a_{i}^{\leq M})^{\dagger}
)
.

For an
m
-mode continuous variable system with Hilbert space
(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}))^{\otimes}\cong L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m}),
we usually consider bare jumps being the annihilation and creation operators on each site, i.e.
\{A^{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\equiv\{a_{i},a^{\dagger}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{m}.
Furthermore, we then consider their Fock basis truncations
a_{i}^{\leq M}
and
\left(a_{i}^{\leq M}\right)^{\dagger}
(c.f. (
4.9
)), whose operator norms satisfy
\|a^{\leq M}_{i}\|\,,\,\|(a^{\leq M}_{i})^{\dagger}\|\leq\sqrt{M}.

For fixed mode
i
these truncated operators can be seen as acting on a
(M+1)
-dimensional space and we, hence, argue in the following how to implement them on a
q=\lceil\log_{2}(M+1)\rceil
qubit device. In binary encoding, the subnormalised operators

a_{i}^{\leq M}/\sqrt{M}
and
(a_{i}^{\leq M})^{\dagger}/\sqrt{M}
are weighted shift operators. A naive
implementation via a flat lookup of the
M+1
coefficients has depth

\mathcal{O}(M)
, up to precision overhead.

However, this can be drastically improved
by noting that the shift part
\ket{n}_{i}\mapsto\ket{n\pm 1}_{i}
is simply reversible
increment/decrement on the
q=\lceil\log_{2}(M+1)\rceil
qubits, while the corresponding
matrix elements are given by the efficiently computable functions

n\mapsto\sqrt{n/M}
and
n\mapsto\sqrt{(n+1)/M}
. Using reversible arithmetic to compute
these coefficients to accuracy
\varepsilon
and loading them into an ancilla rotation
yields a block-encoding of the subnormalized operators

a_{i}^{\leq M}/\sqrt{M}
and
(a_{i}^{\leq M})^{\dagger}/\sqrt{M}
with operator-norm error at
most
\varepsilon
, and with

\mathcal{O}\left(\operatorname{poly}(\log(M),\log(1/\varepsilon))\right)

circuit depth.

Remark 4.22

(Circuit implementation of
e^{itH_{\leq M}}
)
.

For an
m
-mode continuous variable system with Hilbert space
(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}))^{\otimes m}\cong L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m}),
we usually consider the projection onto the system register given by
P_{M}=\pi^{\otimes m}_{M}
with local projections
\pi_{M}=\sum_{n=0}^{M}|n\rangle\!\langle n|
. Due to the tensor product structure of
P_{M}
, we see for
H
being an
\mathcal{O}(1)
degree polynomial in the creation and annihilation operators
a_{i}
and
a^{\dagger}_{i},
that also the truncated Hamiltonian
H_{\leq M}=P_{M}HP_{M}
is a sum of local terms. Encoding each mode separately uses
q=m\left\lceil{\log_{2}(M+1)}\right\rceil
qubits, and each term acts on
\mathcal{O}(\log(M+1))
qubits. Given a term decomposition or block encoding, QSVT techniques provide a circuit implementation of
e^{itH_{\leq M}}
with resource bounds depending on
m
,
M
, the Hamiltonian normalization, and the cost of the supplied coherent access, in addition to
t
and the target accuracy

[
43
,
44
,
45
,
46
,
47
]
.

4.4.2.
Circuit implementation for
integrable filter functions

Combining Proposition
4.17
and Corollary
4.20
with Theorem
4.12
yields the following result on efficient implementation of the Lindblad dynamics for
integrable filters satisfying the weighted-moment and Gevrey assumptions . Recall that the
image of the finite rank projection
P_{M}
involved in the truncation of the Hamiltonian in Section
4.3.2
, i.e.
H_{\leq M}=P_{M}HP_{M},
is referred to as the system register, which governs the size of the quantum computer on which can provide an circuit implementation of the Lindblad dynamics. To make the following results more explicit we consider the scaling
\log(\dim(\operatorname{im}(P_{M})))=\mathcal{O}(|\mathcal{A}|\log(M))
which is the typical scaling required for quantum many-body or multi-mode continuous variable systems.We note, however, that Theorem
4.23

and Corollary
4.25
remain valid for
more general projections
P_{M}
, provided the remaining
assumptions are satisfied. The corresponding number of system qubits, in addition to the ancillas in Corollary
4.20
, is then

\left\lceil\log_{2}\left(\dim\bigl(\operatorname{im}(P_{M})\bigr)\right)\right\rceil.

Theorem 4.23

(Circuit implementation of
e^{t\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}}
for integrable filters)
.

Let
\beta>0,

t\geq 0
and

f\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R})
.
Under the assumptions of Theorem
4.12
, Corollary
4.20
and Proposition
4.17
, with the coherent oracle access specified in Corollary
4.20
, where

(4.50)

\displaystyle M=\Theta\left(\operatorname{poly}\left(\log\left(2+\frac{t\,\mathfrak{c}\,E_{\operatorname{Gibbs}}|\mathcal{A}|}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)\right)

and assume oracle access to a state preparation circuit for

\rho_{M}
satisfying

(4.51)

\rho_{M}:=\frac{P_{M}\rho P_{M}}{\operatorname{Tr}(P_{M}\rho)},\qquad\|P_{M}\rho P_{M}-\rho\|_{1}\leq\varepsilon/8,\quad\text{for some state $\rho$ satisfying }\quad\rho\leq\mathfrak{c}\,\sigma_{\beta}.

Then for
\sigma_{E}\in(0,\infty)
the state
e^{t\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}}(\rho)
can be prepared within
\varepsilon
-trace distance on a quantum computer with
\mathcal{O}\left(|\mathcal{A}|\log(M+1)\right)
system qubits, together with the ancillas specified in Corollary
4.20
, with total Hamiltonian simulation time

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\,(1+t)\,\operatorname{poly}\left(|\mathcal{A}|\,,\,\log\left(2+\frac{\mathfrak{c}\,E_{\operatorname{Gibbs}}}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)\right).

Here,
\mathcal{O}
,
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}
and
\Theta
treat all non-displayed parameters as constant and
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}
additionally supresses subdominant polylogarithmic factors in
t.

Remark 4.24
.

To illustrate the above theorem, let us consider an
m
-mode continuous variable system on the Hilbert space
(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}))^{\otimes m}\cong L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{m}),
choice of bare jumps being
\{A^{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\equiv\{a_{i},a^{\dagger}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{m}
and with local truncations in the Fock basis
a^{\leq M}_{i}
and
(a^{\leq M}_{i})^{\dagger}
being defined in (
4.9
). In this case all assumptions on the jump operators in the above theorem are naturally satisfied for the choice
N_{\mathcal{A}}=N_{\operatorname{tot}}=\sum_{i=1}^{m}a^{\dagger}_{i}a_{i}
at the relevant places in Section
4.3.1
.
Furthermore, as noted in Remark
4.21
, the truncated jumps
a^{\leq M}_{i}
and
(a^{\leq M}_{i})^{\dagger}
can be implemented efficiently by a quantum circuit.

Moreover, for bounded-degree
H
satisfying (
4.13
), the truncation assumptions are verified in Section
4.3.2
. As seen in Remark
4.22
, Hamiltonian simulation with respect to
H_{\leq M}
can also be efficiently implemented on a quantum circuit.

Proof of Theorem
4.23
.

By Theorem
4.12
we know for
\sigma_{E}\in(0,\infty)
fixed that

\displaystyle\left\|\left(e^{t\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}}-e^{t\mathcal{L}^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}}\right)(\rho)\right\|_{1}\leq\varepsilon/4

for some
M
satisfying (
4.50
) and the input-tail
condition (
4.51
). The latter implies

\operatorname{Tr}(P_{M}\rho)>0
(we may take
0<\varepsilon<1
) and

\|\rho_{M}-\rho\|_{1}\leq\|P_{M}\rho P_{M}-\rho\|_{1}+1-\operatorname{Tr}(P_{M}\rho)\leq\varepsilon/4.

Thus input normalization consumes at most one quarter of the error budget.

Furthermore, by Proposition
4.17
we have

\displaystyle\Big\|e^{t\mathcal{L}^{\leq M}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}}-e^{t\mathcal{L}^{\leq M,\mathfrak{n}}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H_{\leq M}}}\Big\|_{1\to 1}\leq\varepsilon/4

for a sufficient quadrature count

\displaystyle\mathfrak{n}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\operatorname{poly}(|\mathcal{A}|,M)+\log\left(2+\frac{|\mathcal{A}|t}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\operatorname{poly}\left(|\mathcal{A}|,\log\left(2+\frac{t\,\mathfrak{c}\,E_{\operatorname{Gibbs}}|\mathcal{A}|}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)\right).

Corollary
4.20
, used with simulation error
\varepsilon/4
, the normalized-input estimate above, and the triangle inequality complete the four
\varepsilon/4
contributions to the error budget.

∎

As a direct consequence of Theorem
4.23
and Corollary
4.13
, we find the following result on efficient Gibbs state preparation under the assumptions of a simple kernel and a positive spectral gap.

Corollary 4.25

(Gibbs state preparation for integrable filter functions)
.

Under the same assumptions as in Theorem
4.23
and assuming additionally that
L_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H},
the self-adjoint generator on
\mathscr{T}_{2}(\mathcal{H})
associated to the Lindbladian
\mathcal{L}_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H},
has a positive spectral gap

\lambda_{2}\equiv\operatorname{gap}\left(L_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}\right)>0
and
\ker L_{\sigma_{E},\widehat{f},H}=\mathbb{C}\sqrt{\sigma_{\beta}}
,
the Gibbs state of the Hamiltonian can be prepared within
\varepsilon
-trace distance on a quantum computer with
\mathcal{O}(|\mathcal{A}|\log(M+1))
system qubits, together with the ancillas specified in Corollary
4.20
, for some

\displaystyle M=\Theta\left(\operatorname{poly}\left(\log\left(2+\frac{\mathfrak{c}\,E_{\operatorname{Gibbs}}|\mathcal{A}|}{\lambda_{2}\varepsilon}\right)\right)\right),

with total Hamiltonian simulation time

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\left(1+\frac{1}{\lambda_{2}}\right)\operatorname{poly}\left(|\mathcal{A}|\,,\,\log\left(2+\frac{\mathfrak{c}E_{\operatorname{Gibbs}}}{\varepsilon}\right)\right)\right).

Here,
\mathcal{O}
,
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}
and
\Theta
treat all non-displayed parameters as constant and
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}
additionally supresses subdominant polylogarithmic factors in
1/\lambda_{2}.

For each fixed
\eta>0
, Lemmas
3.5

and
4.19
verify all filter assumptions
in these implementation results for
\widehat{f}_{\eta}
defined
in (
3.15
). In the one-mode setting this together with Corollary
4.3
gives for the choice
H=h(N)
and bare
jumps

a,a^{\dagger}
the stated Gibbs-state preparation
bounds whenever
M_{\eta}(h)<\infty
, with
M_{\eta}(h)
defined in (
3.16
), and the Hamiltonian, energy,
truncation and oracle assumptions above hold.

Appendix A
Condition

A
for Schrödinger operators

In this section we prove Theorem
2.1
. To verify Condition A for Schrödinger operators, we start by defining the generalized Sobolev spaces

Definition A.1
.

Let
s,\sigma\geq 0
. We define

\mathcal{H}^{s,\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d}):=\Bigl\{f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}):\;\langle x\rangle^{\sigma}f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\quad\langle\xi\rangle^{s}\widehat{f}(\xi)\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\Bigr\},

where
\langle x\rangle:=(1+|x|^{2})^{1/2}
and
\widehat{f}
denotes the Fourier transform of
f
.

Only the oscillator square-root domain is needed here. For

H_{0}=-\Delta+|x|^{2}
, its closed nonnegative quadratic form is

q_{0}[u]=\|\nabla u\|_{2}^{2}+\||x|u\|_{2}^{2},\qquad D(q_{0})=H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\cap\{u:|x|u\in L^{2}\}=\mathcal{H}^{1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

The representation theorem for closed forms therefore gives

D(H_{0}^{1/2})=\mathcal{H}^{1,1}
with equivalent norms

[
35
, Chapter 10]
.
For later use, the standard fractional-power interpolation identity

[
48
, Chapter 4]

gives

[L^{2},D(I+A)]_{\theta}=D((I+A)^{\theta}),\qquad 0<\theta<1,

with graph norms whenever
A\geq 0
is self-adjoint. Thus no general
interpolation identity for intersections is needed. We next recall the
operator domain of
H_{0}
.

Lemma A.2

(Domain of the quantum harmonic oscillator)
.

Let

H_{0}:=-\Delta+|x|^{2}

initially on Schwartz space
\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\subset L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then
H_{0}

is essentially self-adjoint, and its self-adjoint realization satisfies

D(H_{0})=\{u\in H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}):|x|^{2}u\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\}.

Equivalently,

D(H_{0})=\{u\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}):-\Delta u\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\ |x|^{2}u\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\}.

Moreover, the graph norm of
H_{0}
is equivalent to

u\mapsto\|u\|+\|\Delta u\|+\||x|^{2}u\|.

Proof.

For
u\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, the harmonic oscillator is symmetric and essentially self-adjoint by the Hermite basis . We compute

\|H_{0}u\|^{2}=\|-\Delta u+|x|^{2}u\|^{2}=\|\Delta u\|^{2}+\||x|^{2}u\|^{2}-2\Re\langle\Delta u,|x|^{2}u\rangle.

By integration by parts,

-\langle\Delta u,|x|^{2}u\rangle=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\langle\partial_{j}u,\partial_{j}(|x|^{2}u)\rangle=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\langle\partial_{j}u,2x_{j}u+|x|^{2}\partial_{j}u\rangle.

Taking real parts gives

-\Re\langle\Delta u,|x|^{2}u\rangle=\||x|\nabla u\|^{2}+2\Re\sum_{j=1}^{d}\langle\partial_{j}u,x_{j}u\rangle.

Now

2\Re\langle\partial_{j}u,x_{j}u\rangle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}x_{j}\,\partial_{j}(|u|^{2})\,dx=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|u|^{2}\,dx=-\|u\|^{2},

hence

2\Re\sum_{j=1}^{d}\langle\partial_{j}u,x_{j}u\rangle=-d\|u\|^{2}.

Therefore

-\Re\langle\Delta u,|x|^{2}u\rangle=\||x|\nabla u\|^{2}-d\|u\|^{2},

and so

\|H_{0}u\|^{2}=\|\Delta u\|^{2}+\||x|^{2}u\|^{2}+2\||x|\nabla u\|^{2}-2d\|u\|^{2}.

In particular,

\|\Delta u\|^{2}+\||x|^{2}u\|^{2}\leq\|H_{0}u\|^{2}+2d\|u\|^{2}.

Since also

\|H_{0}u\|\leq\|\Delta u\|+\||x|^{2}u\|,

we obtain

\|u\|+\|H_{0}u\|\text{ is equivalent to }\|u\|+\|\Delta u\|+\||x|^{2}u\|\text{ for }u\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Now let
H_{0}
denote the closure of the operator on
\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
By the graph norm equivalence,
u\in D(H_{0})
if and only if there exists a
sequence
u_{n}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that

u_{n}\to u,\qquad\Delta u_{n}\to v,\qquad|x|^{2}u_{n}\to w\quad\text{in }L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

for some
v,w\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Passing to distributions shows that

v=\Delta u
and
w=|x|^{2}u
. Thus

D(H_{0})\subset\{u\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}):\Delta u\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\ |x|^{2}u\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\}.

Conversely, if
u\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
satisfies

\Delta u\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\qquad|x|^{2}u\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),

then
u\in H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Cutoff and mollification give a Schwartz graph-norm approximation. Choosing
u_{n}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with

u_{n}\to u,\qquad\Delta u_{n}\to\Delta u,\qquad|x|^{2}u_{n}\to|x|^{2}u\quad\text{in }L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),

we obtain

H_{0}u_{n}=-\Delta u_{n}+|x|^{2}u_{n}\to-\Delta u+|x|^{2}u\quad\text{in }L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Hence
u\in D(H_{0})
. Therefore

D(H_{0})=\{u\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}):\Delta u\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\ |x|^{2}u\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\}.

The minimal operator
H_{0}
is equal to the maximal operator, thus
H_{0}
is the self-adjoint realization.
Finally, since
u,\Delta u\in L^{2}
implies
u\in H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, this is
equivalent to

D(H_{0})=\{u\in H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}):|x|^{2}u\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\}.

∎

It is well-known that

V\in L^{p_{d}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})+\langle x\rangle^{\alpha}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\text{ with }\alpha<2

is relatively zero-bounded by
[
49
, Theorem X.15]
and
[
50
, Theorem 4.28]
with respect to the harmonic oscillator
H_{0}
.
Here
p_{d}=\max\{2,d/2\}
for
d\neq 4
and
p_{4}>2
for
d=4
; pair Coulomb terms are covered by Hardy’s inequality. Thus, Kato-Rellich’s theorem
[
49
, Theorem X.12]
shows the self-adjointness of
H_{0}+V
on
\mathcal{H}^{2,2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

After a positive shift,
\widetilde{H}=H_{0}+V+\lambda\geq 1
and
I+H_{0}

have the same operator domain with equivalent graph norms. Interpolation
at
\theta=1/2
consequently gives

D(\widetilde{H}^{1/2})=D((I+H_{0})^{1/2})=\mathcal{H}^{1,1}.

The same argument applies to
-\Delta+\nu|x|^{2}+V
for every
\nu>0
.
The creation and annihilation operators map

\mathcal{H}^{1,1}
continuously to
L^{2}
and
\mathcal{H}^{2,2}

continuously to
\mathcal{H}^{1,1}
; the mixed terms are controlled by
the oscillator graph-norm estimate above. This verifies the second
case of Theorem
2.1
, with

\gamma=1/2
and
\mu=1
.

Proposition A.3
.

Let

H=-\Delta+V(x)\quad\text{on }L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),

where
V\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is real-valued and satisfies

V(x)\geq c\langle x\rangle^{r}-C_{0},\qquad c>0,\quad r\geq 1,

as well as

|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}V(x)|\leq C_{\alpha}\langle x\rangle^{r-|\alpha|},\qquad\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{d}.

For
h_{0}\geq C_{0}
, we have

\widetilde{H}:=H+(h_{0}+1)I.

Let
a_{j},a_{j}^{\dagger}
be the annihilation and creation operators.
Then for every
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,

\widetilde{H}^{n}a_{j}\widetilde{H}^{-n-1},\widetilde{H}^{n}a_{j}^{\dagger}\widetilde{H}^{-n-1}\in\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})).

Proof.

We work in the scattering calculus
\Psi^{m,l}_{\mathrm{sc}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Recall
[
51
, (1.3)]
that a smooth function
a(x,\xi)\in S^{m,l}_{\mathrm{sc}}
if

|D_{x}^{\alpha}D_{\xi}^{\beta}a(x,\xi)|\leq C_{\alpha\beta}\langle x\rangle^{l-|\alpha|}\langle\xi\rangle^{m-|\beta|}

for all multi-indices
\alpha,\beta.

Its quantization
[
51
, (1.1)]
defines
\Psi^{m,l}_{\mathrm{sc}}
.

We first show that

\widetilde{H}\in\Psi^{2,r}_{\mathrm{sc}}.

Indeed, its symbol is

\widetilde{p}(x,\xi)=|\xi|^{2}+V(x)+h_{0}+1.

The term
|\xi|^{2}
satisfies

|D_{x}^{\alpha}D_{\xi}^{\beta}|\xi|^{2}|\leq C_{\alpha\beta}\langle\xi\rangle^{2-|\beta|}\leq C_{\alpha\beta}\langle x\rangle^{r-|\alpha|}\langle\xi\rangle^{2-|\beta|},

For
\alpha\neq 0
the differentiated
|\xi|^{2}
term vanishes, and for
V(x)+h_{0}+1
we use the assumption on derivatives of
V
.
By the composition property of the scattering calculus
[
51
, (2.1)]
,

\Psi^{m_{1},l_{1}}_{\mathrm{sc}}\circ\Psi^{m_{2},l_{2}}_{\mathrm{sc}}\subset\Psi^{m_{1}+m_{2},\;l_{1}+l_{2}}_{\mathrm{sc}}.

Since
\widetilde{H}\in\Psi^{2,r}_{\mathrm{sc}}
, an induction gives

(A.1)

\widetilde{H}^{n}\in\Psi^{2n,rn}_{\mathrm{sc}}\qquad\text{for all }n\in\mathbb{N}.

For the inverse estimate use the elliptic sum weight
m(x,\xi)=\langle\xi\rangle^{2}+\langle x\rangle^{r}
. Standard elliptic symbolic calculus gives

(A.2)

\widetilde{H}^{-n-1}\in\operatorname{Op}S(m^{-n-1}),

and
\widetilde{H}^{n}\in\operatorname{Op}S(m^{n})
.

The operator

a_{j}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(x_{j}+\partial_{x_{j}})\text{ and }a_{j}^{\dagger}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(x_{j}-\partial_{x_{j}})

have symbols

b_{j}(x,\xi)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(x_{j}+i\xi_{j})\text{ and }\overline{b_{j}}(x,\xi)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(x_{j}-i\xi_{j}).

A direct inspection gives
a_{j},a_{j}^{\dagger}\in\operatorname{Op}S(\ell)
with
\ell=\langle x\rangle+\langle\xi\rangle
. Hence

\widetilde{H}^{n}a_{j}\widetilde{H}^{-n-1},\ \widetilde{H}^{n}a_{j}^{\dagger}\widetilde{H}^{-n-1}\in\operatorname{Op}S(\ell/m)\subset\Psi^{0,0}_{\mathrm{sc}}.

Since
\ell/m
is bounded for
r\geq 1
, the preceding inclusion in
\Psi^{0,0}_{\mathrm{sc}}
follows directly from the symbol derivative bounds. The standard boundedness
result for the scattering calculus
[
51
, Prop. 3.6]
implies that every operator in

\Psi^{m,l}_{\mathrm{sc}}
with
m\leq 0
and
l\leq 0
is bounded on
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
Therefore

\widetilde{H}^{n}a_{j}\widetilde{H}^{-n-1},\widetilde{H}^{n}a_{j}^{\dagger}\widetilde{H}^{-n-1}\in\mathcal{B}(L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})).

∎
